Appendix for the MPST Calculus

\begin{array}[t]{@{}c@{}}{\tt succ}(\mathsf{n})\downarrow(\mathsf{n}+1)\qquad{\tt neg}(\mathsf{i})\downarrow(-\mathsf{i})\qquad\neg\mathsf{true}\downarrow\mathsf{false}\quad\neg\mathsf{false}\downarrow\mathsf{true}\qquad\mathsf{v}\downarrow\mathsf{v}\\
\\
\frac{\mathsf{e}_{1}\downarrow\mathsf{true}}{\mathsf{e}_{1}\vee\mathsf{e}_{2}\downarrow\mathsf{true}}\qquad\frac{\mathsf{e}_{2}\downarrow\mathsf{true}}{\mathsf{e}_{1}\vee\mathsf{e}_{2}\downarrow\mathsf{true}}\qquad\frac{\mathsf{e}_{1}\downarrow\mathsf{false}\hskip 8.19447pt\mathsf{e}_{2}\downarrow\mathsf{false}}{\mathsf{e}_{1}\vee\mathsf{e}_{2}\downarrow\mathsf{false}}\\
\\
\frac{\mathsf{e}_{1}\downarrow\mathsf{v}_{1}\hskip 8.19447pt\mathsf{e}_{2}\downarrow\mathsf{v}_{2}\hskip 8.19447pt\mathsf{v}_{1}+\mathsf{v}_{1}=\mathsf{v}}{\mathsf{e}_{1}+\mathsf{e}_{2}\downarrow\mathsf{v}}\qquad\frac{\mathsf{e}_{1}\downarrow\mathsf{v}}{\mathsf{e}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{e}_{2}\downarrow\mathsf{v}}\qquad\frac{\mathsf{e}_{2}\downarrow\mathsf{v}}{\mathsf{e}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{e}_{2}\downarrow\mathsf{v}}\qquad\frac{\mathsf{e}\downarrow\mathsf{v}\hskip 8.19447ptE(\mathsf{v})\downarrow\mathsf{v}^{\prime}}{E(\mathsf{e})\downarrow\mathsf{v}^{\prime}}\end{array}

Table 4
.
Expression evaluation.

An
evaluation context

E

is an expression with exactly one hole. Table
4
defines the evaluation rules for expressions.

Appendix B
Appendix for Complexity of Multiparty Subtyping Checking

Lemma B.1.

If
{\sf T}^{\prime}
is reachable from
{\sf T}
in the type graph

\mathbb{G}({\sf T})
then
{\sf T}^{\prime}\in{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})
.

Proof.

By induction on the length of the path from
{\sf T}^{\prime}
to
{\sf T}
, and matching this with the appropriate rule in Definition
3.2
.
∎

Lemma B.2.

|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})|=\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf T}|\right)
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
{\sf T}
. We will show that
|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})|\leq|{\sf T}|
.

Case
{\sf T}=\mathtt{end}
or
{\sf T}=\mathbf{t}
: trivial.

Case
{\sf T}=\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}^{\prime}
:
|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})|=|\{\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}^{\prime}\}\cup\{{\sf T}^{\prime\prime}[\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}^{\prime}/\mathbf{t}]\mid{\sf T}^{\prime\prime}\in{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T}^{\prime})\}|\leq 1+|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T}^{\prime})|=|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})|
.

Case
{\sf T}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T}^{\prime}
:
|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})|=|\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T}^{\prime}\}\cup{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T}^{\prime})|\leq 1+|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T}^{\prime})|=|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})|
.

Case
{\sf T}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{{\sf T}_{1},\dots,{\sf T}_{n}\}
:
{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})=|\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{{\sf T}_{1},\dots,{\sf T}_{n}\}\}\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T}_{i})|\leq 1+\sum_{i=1}^{n}|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T}_{i})|\leq 1+\sum_{i=1}^{n}|{\sf T}_{i}|=|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})|
.

Other cases: similar.
∎

Lemma B.3.

The number of edges in the type graph for
{\sf T}
is
O(|{\sf T}|)
.

Proof.

Define the following function, which is the out-degree of an unfolded type:
\rm{od}(\mathbf{t})=0
;
\rm{od}(\mathtt{end})=1
;
\rm{od}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T})=\rm{od}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T})=1
; and
\rm{od}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I})=\rm{od}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I})=|I|
.

Then, the number of edges in the LTS is

\sum_{U\text{ reachable from }T}\rm{od}({\mathsf{unfold}}({U}))\leq\sum_{U\in{\rm{Sub}}(T)}\rm{od}(U)
.

Let
f({\sf T})=\sum_{{\sf T}^{\prime}\in{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})}\rm{od}({\sf T}^{\prime})
.

We prove that
f(T)\leq 2|{\sf T}|-1
, by structural induction on
{\sf T}
.

Case
T=\mathtt{end}
, or
T=\mathbf{t}
. Then
{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})=\{{\sf T}\}
, so
f({\sf T})\leq 2|{\sf T}|-1
.

Case
{\sf T}=\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}^{\prime}
. Then
f({\sf T})=\rm{od}({\sf T})+f({\sf T}^{\prime})
. By the inductive hypothesis,
f({\sf T}^{\prime})\leq 2|{\sf T}^{\prime}|-1
. By definition,
\rm{od}({\sf T})=0
, so
\sum_{{\sf T}^{\prime\prime}\in{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})}\rm{od}({\sf T}^{\prime\prime})\leq 2|{\sf T}^{\prime}|-1\leq 2|{\sf T}|-1
.

Case
{\sf T}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T}^{\prime}
,
T={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T}^{\prime}
. Then
f({\sf T})=1+f({\sf T}^{\prime})
. By the inductive hypothesis,
f({\sf T}^{\prime})\leq 2|{\sf T}^{\prime}|-1
. By definition,
\rm{od}({\sf T})=1
, so
f({\sf T})\leq 2|{\sf T}|-1
.

Case
T={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
,
T={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
. Then
f({\sf T})=\rm{od}({\sf T})+\sum_{i\in I}f({\sf T}_{i})
. By the inductive hypothesis,
f({\sf T}_{i})\leq 2|{\sf T}_{i}|-1
. By definition,
\rm{od}({\sf T})=|I|
, so
f({\sf T})\leq|I|+\sum_{i\in I}(2|{\sf T}_{i}|-1)=2\sum_{i\in I}|T_{i}|-1\leq 2|{\sf T}|-1
.
∎

Corollary B.4.

The number of nodes in the type graph of
{\sf T}
is
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf T}|\right)
.

See
3.9

Proof.

Note that
\leqslant
is the largest type simulation, as all type simulations are consistent with the rules in Definition
3.5
. Thus the forward direction is immediate. For the backward direction, if
{\sf T}_{1}\mathcal{R}{\sf T}_{2}
then
\mathcal{R}
is consistent with the subtyping rules, so
{\sf T}_{1}\leqslant{\sf T}_{2}
.
∎

B.1.
Proof of Theorem
3.11

To help with the proofs of the complexity results in this section, we first define
nesting depth
:

Definition B.5 (Nesting depth).

The nesting depth of a type is defined inductively:

\displaystyle{\rm{nd}}(\mathtt{end})={\rm{nd}}(\mathbf{t})

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle{\rm{nd}}(\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T})

\displaystyle={\rm{nd}}({\sf T})+1

\displaystyle{\rm{nd}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T})={\rm{nd}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T})

\displaystyle={\rm{nd}}({\sf T})+1

\displaystyle{\rm{nd}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I})={\rm{nd}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I})

\displaystyle=\max(\{{\rm{nd}}({\sf T}_{i})\mid i\in I\})+1

We first give an algorithm which is worst-case exponential, taken from Ghilezan
et al.

(
Ghilezan et al., 2019
)
, which is the algorithm in
(
Gay and Hole, 2005
)
adapted to the multiparty setting. We adapt the syntax to more closely match ours: in particular, we have separate constructs for sending and selection, as well as receiving and branching. The algorithm involves rules in Figure
10
. These rules prove judgements of the form
\Theta\vdash{\sf T}\leq{\sf T}^{\prime}
, which intuitively means “assuming the relations in
\Theta
, we may conclude that
{\sf T}
is a subtype of
{\sf T}^{\prime}
”.

[
Alg-Assump
]

({\sf T},{\sf T}^{\prime})\in\Theta
\Theta\vdash{\sf T}\leq{\sf T}^{\prime}

[
Alg-End
]

\Theta\vdash\mathtt{end}\leq\mathtt{end}

[
Alg-RecL
]

\Theta\cup\{(\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T},{\sf T}^{\prime})\}\vdash{\sf T}[\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}/\mathbf{t}]\leq{\sf T}^{\prime}
\Theta\vdash\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}\leq{\sf T}^{\prime}

[
Alg-RecR
]

\Theta\cup\{({\sf T},\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}^{\prime})\}\vdash{\sf T}\leq{\sf T}^{\prime}[\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}^{\prime}/\mathbf{t}]
\Theta\vdash{\sf T}\leq\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}^{\prime}

[
Alg-In
]

\Theta\vdash{\sf T}_{1}\leq{\sf T}^{\prime}_{1}
\Theta\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}_{1}}\leq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}^{\prime}_{1}}

[
Alg-Out
]

\Theta\vdash{\sf T}_{1}\leq{\sf T}^{\prime}_{1}
\Theta\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}_{1}}\leq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}^{\prime}_{1}}

[
Alg-Bra
]

I\subseteq J
\forall i\in I.\>\Theta\vdash{\sf T}_{i}\leq{\sf T}^{\prime}_{i}
\Theta\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{j}:{\sf T}_{j}\}_{j\in J}\leq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I}

[
Alg-Sel
]

I\subseteq J
\forall i\in I.\>\Theta\vdash{\sf T}_{i}\leq{\sf T}^{\prime}_{i}
\Theta\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\leq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{j}:{\sf T}_{j}\}_{j\in J}

Figure 10.
Algorithmic rules for subtyping, from
(
Ghilezan et al., 2019
, Table 6)
(syntax slightly modified).

Ghilezan
et al.

(
Ghilezan et al., 2019
, Theorem 3.26)
prove soundness and completeness of this type system:

Theorem B.6.

\emptyset\vdash{\sf T}\leq{\sf T}^{\prime}
iff
{\sf T}\leq{\sf T}^{\prime}
.

Using this theorem, we can give an algorithm for subtyping of session types. The algorithm tries to build the proof tree bottom-up, using rule
[
Alg-Assump
]
if possible. Note that the rule taken at each step is deterministic (if we give
[
Alg-RecL
]
higher priority that
[
Alg-RecR
]
). By the above theorem, this algorithm is correct.

We adapt the results in
(
Udomsrirungruang and Yoshida, 2024
)
to prove the following complexity results of this algorithm.
We take
n
=
|{\sf T}|+|{\sf T}|^{\prime}
where the input is
{\sf T},{\sf T}^{\prime}
:

B.1.1.
Upper bound

Theorem B.7.

The upper bound of the worst case complexity of the subtyping algorithm in
(
Ghilezan et al., 2019
)
is
n^{\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(n^{3}\right)}
.

Proof.

We proceed similarly to
(
Udomsrirungruang and Yoshida, 2024
, Theorem 3.6)
. First, note that all types appearing in the proof tree of
\emptyset\vdash{\sf T}_{1}\leq{\sf T}_{2}
are members of
{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T}_{1})\cup{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T}_{2})
.
Furthermore, if
\Theta^{\prime\prime}\vdash{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime\prime}\leq{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
appears above
\Theta^{\prime}\vdash{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}\leq{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}
, then either
|\Theta^{\prime\prime}|>|\Theta^{\prime}|
or
{\rm{nd}}({\sf T}_{1}^{\prime\prime})<{\rm{nd}}({\sf T}_{1}^{\prime})
; this can be proved by induction on the proof tree. Thus, in any vertical path in the proof tree, the pairs
(\Theta^{\prime},{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime})
are distinct.
Therefore, in any judgement
\Theta\vdash{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}\leq{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}
in the proof tree, there are only
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(n^{2}\right)
possible pairs that can be in
\Theta
. Furthermore, there are only
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(n\right)
possible values of
{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}
, and thus
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(n\right)
possible values of
{\rm{nd}}({\sf T}_{1}^{\prime})
. Therefore, the height of the proof tree is bounded by
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(n^{3}\right)
.
As the branching factor is
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(n\right)
, we conclude that the total number of nodes in the proof tree is
n^{\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(n^{3}\right)}
. Each node requires
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(1\right)
time to process.
∎

B.1.2.
Lower bound

We exhibit an input to Ghilezan
et al.
’s subtyping algorithm where the runtime is exponential.

Definition B.8.

Define the (not necessarily closed) type fragments:

\displaystyle{\sf T}^{\mathsf{af}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{t}

\displaystyle{\sf T}^{\mathsf{af}}_{r}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{1}:{\sf T}^{\mathsf{af}}_{r-1},l_{2}:\mu\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{r-1}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{bf}}_{r-1}\}

\displaystyle(r>0)

\displaystyle{\sf T}^{\mathsf{bf}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{t}

\displaystyle{\sf T}^{\mathsf{bf}}_{r}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{1}:{\sf T}^{\mathsf{bf}}_{r-1},l_{2}:{\sf T}^{\mathsf{c}}\}

\displaystyle(r>0)

\displaystyle{\sf T}^{\mathsf{c}}

\displaystyle=\mu\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{1}:\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime},l_{2}:\mathbf{t}^{\prime\prime}\}

Let
{\sf T}_{k}=\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{k}
. Define the (closed) types:
{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{r,k}={\sf T}^{\mathsf{af}}_{r}[{\sf T}_{k}/\mathbf{t}]
,
{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{r,k}={\sf T}^{\mathsf{bf}}_{r}[{\sf T}_{k}/\mathbf{t}]
.

Note that in the above definition, there are no bound occurrences of
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}_{k}
, even though we bind it in a
\mu
.
This ensures that all nodes in the statement of Lemma
B.9
are unique.

Lemma B.9.

For every sequence
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{l}(0\leq l<k)
such that
0\leq\alpha_{i}<k-i
:

(1)

\displaystyle\small\left.\begin{aligned} &\{{\sf T}_{k}\leq{\sf T}_{k+1},{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{k,k}\leq T_{k+1}\}\\
\cup&\{\mu\mathbf{t}_{\alpha_{i}}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{i}},k}\leq\mu\mathbf{t}_{\alpha_{i}+i}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{i}+i},{k+1}}\mid 1\leq i\leq l\}\\
\cup&\{{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{i}},k}\leq\mu\mathbf{t}_{\alpha_{i}+i}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{i}+i},{k+1}}\mid 1\leq i\leq l\}\\
\cup&\{{\sf T}_{k}\leq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{i,{k+1}}\mid 1\leq i\leq l\}\\
\cup&\{{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{k-i},k}\leq{\sf T}_{k+1}\mid 1\leq i\leq l\}\end{aligned}\right\}

\displaystyle\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{k-l},k}\leq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{k+1},{k+1}}

is derivable from
\emptyset\vdash T_{k}\leq T_{k+1}
as the root.

Proof.

By induction on
l
. For some
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{l}
, let
\mathcal{S}_{l}
be the set of inferences on the left side of (
1
).

The base case
l=0
is simple:
\mathcal{S}_{0}=\{{\sf T}_{k}\leq{\sf T}_{k+1},{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{k,k}\leq{\sf T}_{k+1}\}
. The corresponding proof tree is

[
Alg-RecL
]

[
Alg-RecR
]

\mathcal{S}_{0}\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{k,k}\leq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{k+1},{k+1}}
{\sf T}_{k}\leq{\sf T}_{k+1}\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{k,k}\leq{\sf T}_{k+1}

\emptyset\vdash{\sf T}_{k}\leq{\sf T}_{k+1}

For the inductive step, we will build the tree starting from

\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{k-(l-1)},k}\leq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}{{k+1},k}
, for
l>0
,
using the inductive hypothesis.
The following proof tree works, taking

\mathcal{C}_{1}=\mu\mathbf{t}_{\alpha_{l}}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{l}},k}\leq\mu\mathbf{t}_{\alpha_{l}+l}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{l}+l},{k+1}},\>\mathcal{C}_{2}={\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{l}},k}\leq\mu\mathbf{t}_{\alpha_{l}+l}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{l}+l},{k+1}},

\mathcal{C}_{3}={\sf T}_{k}\leq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{l,{k+1}},\>\mathcal{C}_{4}={\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{k-l},k}\leq{\sf T}_{k+1}
:

[
Alg-Sel
]

[
Alg-Sel
]

[
Alg-Sel
]

[
Alg-RecL
]

[
Alg-RecR
]

[
Alg-Sel
]

[
Alg-Sel
]

[
Alg-RecL
]

[
Alg-Sel
]

[
Alg-Sel
]

[
Alg-RecR
]

\dots
\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\cup\{\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},\mathcal{C}_{3},\mathcal{C}_{4}\}\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{k-l},k}\leq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{k+1},{k+1}}
\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\cup\{\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},\mathcal{C}_{3}\}\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{k-l},k}\leq{\sf T}_{k+1}={\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{0,{k+1}}

\dots
\vdots

\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\cup\{\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},\mathcal{C}_{3}\}\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{k,k}\leq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{l,{k+1}}

\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\cup\{\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}\}\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{0,k}={\sf T}_{k}\leq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{l,{k+1}}

\dots
\vdots

\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\cup\{\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}\}\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{l}},k}\leq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{l}+l},{k+1}}

\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\cup\{\mathcal{C}_{1}\}\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{l}},k}\leq\mu\mathbf{t}_{\alpha_{l}}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{l}+1},{k+1}}

\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\vdash\mu\mathbf{t}_{\alpha_{l}}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{l}},k}\leq\mu\mathbf{t}_{\alpha_{l}+l}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{b}}_{{\alpha_{l}+l},{k+1}}

\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{\alpha_{l}+1},k}\leq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{\alpha_{l}+l+1},{k+1}}

\dots
\vdots

\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\vdash{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{k-(l-1)},k}\leq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{a}}_{{k+1},{k+1}}

Observing that
\mathcal{S}_{l}=\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\cup\{\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},\mathcal{C}_{3},\mathcal{C}_{4}\}
finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma B.10.

Using Definition
B.8
,
{\sf T}_{k}\simeq{\sf T}^{\mathsf{c}}
for all
k\geq 0
.

Proof.

All subformulas of
{\sf T}_{k}
have shape
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{1}:{\sf T}^{(1)},l_{2}:{\sf T}^{(2)}\}
,
for some
{\sf T}^{(1)},{\sf T}^{(2)}\in{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T}_{k})
.
Therefore,
\{({\sf T},{\sf T}^{\mathsf{c}})\mid{\sf T}\in{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T}_{k})\}
and
\{({\sf T}^{\mathsf{c}},{\sf T})\mid{\sf T}\in{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T}_{k})\}
are type simulations.
∎

Corollary B.11.

T_{k}\lesssim T_{k+1}
.

Proof.

Follows from transitivity of
\simeq
.
∎

Theorem B.12.

The lower bound of the worst case complexity of the subtyping algorithm in
(
Ghilezan et al., 2019
)
is
\Omega\negmedspace\left((\sqrt{n})!\right)
.

Proof.

Consider the input
({\sf T}_{k},{\sf T}_{k+1})
. By Corollary
B.11
,

{\sf T}_{k}\leqslant{\sf T}_{k+1}
, and so the algorithm in
(
Ghilezan et al., 2019
)
will build
a full proof tree of
\emptyset\vdash{\sf T}_{k}\leqslant{\sf T}_{k+1}
.
By Lemma
B.9
, there is at least one (distinct) node in the proof tree for each
sequence
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{l}
(
0\leq l<k
,
0\leq\alpha_{i}<k-i
), of which there are
k!
.
We have
n=\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(k^{2}\right)
, so the number of nodes is at least
\Omega\negmedspace\left((\sqrt{n})!\right)
.
∎

Combining the upper and lower bounds yields the stated result.

See
3.11

Appendix C
Appendix for Complexity of Projection Algorithms

C.1.
Appendix for Complexity of Inductive Projection with Plain Merging

Remark C.1 (Merging and type safety).

Scalas and Yoshida
(
Scalas and Yoshida, 2019
)
have discovered that the proofs of
type safety in the literature which use the full merging
is flawed (while the proof using the plain merging

(
Honda et al., 2008
;
Honda et al., 2016
;
Coppo et al., 2015
)
is
not
flawed).
After this paper, researchers wrongly believed
that the end-point projection is unsound. A recent paper
by Yoshida and Hou
(
Yoshida and Hou, 2024
)

has corrected this misunderstanding, proposing a new general proof technique
for type soundness of multiparty session
\pi
-calculus, which uses an
association relation between a global type and its end-point
projection.

Lemma C.1.

{|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf G})|}=\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\right)
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
{\sf G}
, similarly to Lemma
B.2
.
∎

Lemma C.2.

Under plain merging,
|{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}|\leq|{\sf G}|
.

Proof.

Straightforward; by induction on the structure of
{\sf G}
.
∎

The following theorem completes the proof of Theorem
4.9
.

Theorem C.3.

There exists an algorithm for inductive projection with plain merging
with worst-case time complexity
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left({|{\sf G}|}\log{|{\sf G}|}\right)
.

Proof.

First, note that by keeping track of the free variables, we may check every syntactic subterm of
{\sf G}
for closedness in
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(n\right)
time. We perform this as a first pass. Then, our algorithm will perform the projection in the same recursive sense as in Definition
4.5
, except for the aforementioned checking of closedness. In particular, computing
{{{\sqcap}}}_{i\in I}{{\sf G}_{i}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
will be a syntactic equality check. First, we prove the result for global types where the projection is defined.

Claim C.1.

The algorithm computes
{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
in
2|{\sf G}|\log|{\sf G}|
time, if
{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
is defined.

Proof.

We prove that the algorithm computes
{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
in
2|{\sf G}|(\log|{\sf G}|+1)
time, if
{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
is defined.
We use base-
2
logarithms for convenience:

Case
{\sf G}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf G}^{\prime}
. We have
|{\sf G}|=|{\sf G}^{\prime}|+1
; by the inductive hypothesis the time taken, including a constant time overhead, is
2|{\sf G}^{\prime}|(\log|{\sf G}^{\prime}|+1)+1\leq 2|{\sf G}|(\log|{\sf G}|+1)
.

Case
{\sf G}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\notin\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\}
. If
I=\{i\}
then by the same logic as above, we compute
{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
in time
2|{\sf G}^{\prime}|(\log|{\sf G}^{\prime}|+1)+1\leq 2|{\sf G}|(\log|{\sf G}|+1)
.

Otherwise,
|I|\geq 2
. Let
n_{i}=|{\sf G}_{i}|
and
{\sf T}_{i}={{\sf G}_{i}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
. Then the projections of
{\sf G}_{i}
take time
2n_{i}\log n_{i}
; the merging is a syntactic equality check which takes time
\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{i}|
. We are guaranteed that
{\sf T}_{i}={\sf T}
because of the plain merge.

Let
x\in I
be such that
n_{x}
is maximum. Thus, for
i\neq x
,
n_{i}\leq\frac{n}{2}
so
\log n_{i}+1\leq\log n
.
The time taken for recursive projection is
\sum_{i\in I}\left(2n_{i}(\log n_{i}+1)\right)
and merging is
\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{i}|
. Hence we have:

\displaystyle\>\underbrace{\sum_{i\in I}\left(2n_{i}(\log n_{i}+1)\right)}_{\small\text{recursive projection}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{i}|}_{\small\text{merging}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{i\in I}\left(2n_{i}(\log n_{i}+1)\right)+|I|\cdot|{\sf T}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>2n_{x}(\log{n_{x}}+1)+\sum_{i\in I\setminus\{x\}}\left(2n_{i}(\log n_{i}+1)+2|{\sf T}|\right)

(because
|I|\geq 2
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>2n_{x}(\log{n_{x}}+1)+\sum_{i\in I\setminus\{x\}}\left(2n_{i}(\log n_{i}+1)+2n_{i}\right)

(by Lemma
C.2
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>2n_{x}(\log n+1)+\sum_{i\in I\setminus\{x\}}\left(2n_{i}(\log n+1)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>2(\log n+1)\sum_{i\in I}n_{i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>2n(\log n+1)

Case
{\sf G}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\in\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\}
. Then the time taken for the recursive projections (using the same notation as the above case) is
\sum_{i\in I}\left(2n_{i}(\log n_{i}+1)\right)\leq 2n(\log n+1)
.

Case
{\sf G}=\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf G}^{\prime}
. Let
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{\sf G}^{\prime}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
. We have
|{\sf T}|\leq|{\sf T}^{\prime}|+1
. Checking for closedness was precomputed, thus the total time is
2|{\sf T}^{\prime}|(\log|{\sf T}^{\prime}|+1)+1\leq 2|{\sf T}|(\log|{\sf T}|+1)\leq 2|{\sf G}|(\log|{\sf G}|+1)
.

Case
{\sf G}=\mathtt{end}
and
{\sf G}=\mathbf{t}
. Both are projectable in constant time, and so it is bounded by
{\sf G}(\log|{\sf G}|+1)=1
.
∎

Claim C.2.

The algorithm computes

{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
in

2|{\sf G}|\log|{\sf G}|+|{\sf G}|
, if
{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
is undefined.

Proof.

Most reasoning is identical to the above proof, except for the case where an unprojectability error is found, i.e.
{\sf G}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\notin\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\}
,
|I|\geq 2
,
{{\sf G}_{i}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
is defined for all
i
, and
{{{\sqcap}}}_{i\in I}{{\sf G}_{i}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
is undefined.
In this case we cannot rely on the fact that
|{\sf T}_{j}|<n_{i}
for all
i
,
j
, which was necessary for the above proof.
Instead we prove the following bound:
\sum_{i\in I}\left(2n_{i}\log n_{i}\right)+\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{i}|\leq 2\log n\cdot\sum_{i\in I}n_{i}+\sum_{i\in I}|{{\sf G}_{i}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}|\leq 2n\log n+\sum_{i\in I}|{{\sf G}_{i}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}|\leq 2n\log n+n
.
∎

This concludes the proofs.
∎

C.2.
Appendix for Complexity of Inductive Projection with Full Merging

Lemma C.4.

If
{\sf T}_{1}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}={\sf T}
, then
|{\sf T}|<|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
{\sf T}_{1}
.

Case
{\sf T}_{1}=\mathbf{t}
or
{\sf T}_{1}=\mathtt{end}
. Then
{\sf T}_{2}={\sf T}
, so
|{\sf T}|=1<2=|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|
.

Case
{\sf T}_{1}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}}
. Then
{\sf T}_{2}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}}
, so
|{\sf T}|=1+|{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}|<1+|{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}|+|{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}|<|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|
.

Case
{\sf T}_{1}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}}
. Similar to the above.

Case
{\sf T}_{1}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\}_{i\in I}
. Then
{\sf T}_{2}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}\}_{i\in I}
, so
|{\sf T}|=1+\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|<1+\sum_{i\in I}(|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|+|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|)<1+|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|
.

Case
{\sf T}_{1}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\}_{i\in I}
. Then
{\sf T}_{2}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{2}^{(j)}\}_{j\in J}
, so
|{\sf T}|=1+\sum_{i\in I\cap J}\left({\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}\right)+\sum_{i\in I\setminus J}\left({\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\right)+\sum_{i\in J\setminus I}\left({\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}\right)<1+\sum_{i\in I}({\sf T}_{1}^{(i)})+\sum_{i\in J}({\sf T}_{2}^{(i)})<|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|
.
∎

By repeated application of the above lemma, we have:

Corollary C.5.

If
{{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I}{\sf T}_{i}={\sf T}
and
{\sf T}
is defined then
|{\sf T}|<\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{i}|
.

Lemma C.6.

Under full merging,
|{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}|\leq|{\sf G}|
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
{\sf G}
.

Case
{\sf G}=\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\right)
where
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\notin\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\}
.
Then, using Corollary
C.5
and the inductive hypothesis,

|{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}|=1+{{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I}{{\sf G}_{i}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}<1+\sum_{i\in I}|{{\sf G}_{i}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}|<1+\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf G}_{i}|=|{\sf G}|
.

All other cases are trivial.
∎

See
4.11

Proof.

To allow for the complexity logarithmic in
|{\sf T}_{1}|
, in our algorithm (and other algorithms that use this lemma), we will represent local types as follows:

•

\mathbf{t}
and
\mathtt{end}
are primitive.

•

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}}
contain a pointer to
{\sf T}
.

•

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is represented as a (sorted) list of
\langle l_{i},\text{pointer to }{\sf T}_{i}\rangle
pairs.

•

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is represented as a balanced binary search tree (BST) with keys
l_{i}
and values as pointers to
{\sf T}_{i}
. This is done by first fixing a total ordering on labels consistently across the algorithm.

This differs from the naïve representation only in the representation of branching: instead of a list of branches, we will use a BST.

Global types will be represented in the usual way, as syntactic trees.

By structural induction on
{\sf T}_{1}
. We will show that, up to constant factors, the merge can be computed in time at most
2(|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|-|{\sf T}|)-1+(|{\sf T}_{2}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}|
. Note that this is positive by Lemma
C.4
.

Case
{\sf T}_{1}=\mathbf{t}
or
{\sf T}_{1}=\mathtt{end}
. Then
{\sf T}_{2}={\sf T}
. This can be checked in time
1=2(|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|-|{\sf T}|)-1+(|{\sf T}_{2}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}|
.

Case
{\sf T}_{1}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}}
,
{\sf T}_{2}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}}
. Then it suffices to find
{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}
. By the inductive hypothesis this takes

2(|{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}|+|{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}|-|{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}|)-1+(|{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}|
time.
Adding a constant overhead of
1
, the total time is

2(|{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}|+|{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}|-|{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}|)+(|{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}|=2(|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|-|{\sf T}|)-2+(|{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}|<2(|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|-|{\sf T}|)-1+(|{\sf T}_{2}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}|
.

Case
{\sf T}_{1}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}_{1}^{\prime}}
,
{\sf T}_{2}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{{\sf T}_{2}^{\prime}}
. Similar to the above.

Case
{\sf T}_{1}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\}_{i\in I}
,
{\sf T}_{2}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}\}_{i\in I}
. Then it suffices to find
{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}
for each
i\in I
. By the inductive hypothesis this takes time:

\displaystyle\>\sum_{i\in I}\left(2(|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|+|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|)-1+(|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>2\left(\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|+\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|\right)-|I|+\sum_{i\in I}(|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>2(|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|-|{\sf T}|)-|I|-2+\sum_{i\in I}(|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}|

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\>2(|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|-|{\sf T}|)-|I|-1+(|{\sf T}_{2}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}|

Adding a linear overhead in the number of branches,
|I|
, yields the result.

Case
{\sf T}_{1}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\}_{i\in I}
,
{\sf T}_{2}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{2}^{(j)}\}_{j\in J}
. We do the following:

•

Start with the BST for
{\sf T}_{1}
, containing
\{l_{i}\mapsto{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\mid i\in I\}
. Call this tree
\mathbb{T}
.

•

Iterate over the BST for
{\sf T}_{2}
. For each item
l_{j}\mapsto{\sf T}_{2}^{(j)}
:

–

If the key
l_{j}
is present in
\mathbb{T}
, with value
{\sf T}_{1}^{(j)}
, then overwrite the value with
{\sf T}_{1}^{(j)}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{(j)}
.

–

Otherwise, add a new key-value pair
l_{j}\mapsto{\sf T}_{2}^{(j)}
to

\mathbb{T}
.

It follows that the tree
\mathbb{T}
at the end of this process is the tree representing the type
{\sf T}={\sf T}_{1}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{2}
. The time taken to perform this operation is the sum of the time taken for the merging of branches in
\{l_{i}\mid i\in I\cap J\}
, as well as the overhead of the
|J|
tree operations:

\displaystyle\>\underbrace{\sum_{i\in I\cap J}\left(2(|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|+|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.08331pt\hskip-3.08331pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.08331pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.33682pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.08331pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|)-1+(|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|\right)}_{\text{merging}}+\underbrace{|J|\log|{\sf T}_{1}|}_{\text{overhead}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>\sum_{i\in I\cap J}\left(2(|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|+|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.08331pt\hskip-3.08331pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.08331pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.33682pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.08331pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|)-1+(|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}|\right)+|J|\log|{\sf T}_{1}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>2\left(\sum_{i\in I\cap J}|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|+\sum_{i\in I\cap J}|{\sf T}_{2}^{(j)}|-\sum_{i\in I\cap J}|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.08331pt\hskip-3.08331pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.08331pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.33682pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.08331pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-|I\cap J|+\log|{\sf T}_{1}|\cdot\left(\sum_{i\in I\cap J}|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-|I\cap J|+|J|\right)

We have

\displaystyle\>\sum_{i\in I\cap J}|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|+\sum_{i\in I\cap J}|{\sf T}_{2}^{(j)}|-\sum_{i\in I\cap J}|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.08331pt\hskip-3.08331pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.08331pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.33682pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.08331pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|+\sum_{i\in J}|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-\left(\sum_{i\in I\cap J}|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.08331pt\hskip-3.08331pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.08331pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.33682pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.08331pt}}{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|+\sum_{i\in I\setminus J}|{\sf T}_{1}^{(i)}|+\sum_{i\in J\setminus I}|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|-|{\sf T}|-1

and

\displaystyle\>\sum_{i\in I\cap J}|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|-|I\cap J|+|J|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{i\in I\cap J}|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|+|J\setminus I|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>\sum_{i\in J}|{\sf T}_{2}^{(i)}|

\displaystyle\text{because }|T_{2}^{(i)}|\geq 1

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>|{\sf T}_{2}|-1

Thus the total time is less than
2(|{\sf T}_{1}|+|{\sf T}_{2}|-|{\sf T}|)-2-|I\cap J|+(|{\sf T}_{2}|-1)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{1}|
, as desired.
∎

Corollary C.7.

If
{{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I}{\sf T}_{i}={\sf T}
and
{\sf T}
is defined then
{\sf T}
can be computed in time

\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(\sum_{i\in I}{|{\sf T}_{i}|}-|{\sf T}|+\log\left(\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{i}|\right)\cdot\sum_{i\in I\setminus\{j\}}|{\sf T}_{i}|\right)
, for any
j\in I
.

Proof.

Our algorithm proceeds as follows:

{\sf T}\leftarrow{\sf T}_{j}

for

i\in I\setminus\{j\}

do

{\sf T}\leftarrow{\sf T}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{i}

end

for

return

{\sf T}

Taking
I=\{j,a_{1},\dots,a_{k}\}
, label the
m
-th intermediate result
{\sf T}^{\prime}_{m}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{j\in\{x,a_{1},\dots,a_{m}\}}{\sf T}_{i}
.
By Lemma
C.6
,
|{\sf T}^{\prime}_{m}|\leq\left(\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{i}|\right)
.
Then by the above lemma, calculating
{\sf T}^{\prime}_{m-1}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{m}
takes time proportional to

|{\sf T}^{\prime}_{m-1}|+|{\sf T}_{m}|-|{\sf T}^{\prime}_{m}|+|{\sf T}_{m}|\log|{\sf T}^{\prime}_{m-1}|\leq\>|{\sf T}^{\prime}_{m-1}|+|{\sf T}_{m}|-|{\sf T}^{\prime}_{m}|+\log\left(\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{i}|\right)\cdot|{\sf T}_{m}|

Using the facts that
{\sf T}_{0}^{\prime}={\sf T}_{j}
and
{\sf T}_{k}^{\prime}={\sf T}
, summing the above for
1\leq m\leq k
yields the desired result.
∎

The following theorem completes the proof of Theorem
4.12
.

Theorem C.8.

There exists an algorithm for inductive projection with full merging
with worst-case time complexity

\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left({|{\sf G}|}\log^{2}{|{\sf G}|}\right)
.

Proof.

Similarly to the plain merging algorithm, we start by checking every syntactic subterm for closedness in linear time.
Then, we show inductively that (up to constant factors) we can compute
{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
in time
|{\sf G}|\log^{2}|{\sf G}|+2|{\sf G}|-|{\sf T}|
, where
{\sf T}={{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
.

For convenience, define
|{\sf T}|=0
if
{\sf T}
is undefined. We use base-
2
logarithms for ease of explanations.

Case
{\sf G}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf G}^{\prime}
. We have
|{\sf G}^{\prime}|+1=|{\sf G}|
. Define
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{\sf G}^{\prime}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
; we have
|{\sf T}|\leq|{\sf T}^{\prime}|+1
. Then the time taken is
1+|{\sf G}^{\prime}|\log^{2}|{\sf G}^{\prime}|+2|{\sf G}^{\prime}|-|{\sf T}^{\prime}|\leq|{\sf G}^{\prime}|\log^{2}|{\sf G}^{\prime}|+2|{\sf G}|-|{\sf T}|\leq|{\sf G}|\log^{2}|{\sf G}|+2|{\sf G}|-|{\sf T}|
.

Case
{\sf G}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\notin\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\}
. Denote
n_{i}=|{\sf G}_{i}|
. Let
m\in I
be such that
|G_{m}|
is maximised. Let
{\sf T}_{i}={{\sf G}_{i}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
. Then the time taken is

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sum_{i\in I}\left(n_{i}\log^{2}n_{i}+2n_{i}-|{\sf T}_{i}|\right)}_{\small\text{recursive projection}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{i}|-|{\sf T}|+\log\left(\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{i}|\right)\cdot\log|{\sf T}_{m}|\sum_{i\in I\setminus\{m\}}|{\sf T}_{i}|}_{\small\text{merging}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{i\in I}\left(n_{i}\log^{2}n_{i}\right)+\log\left(\sum_{i\in I}|{\sf T}_{i}|\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{i\in I\setminus\{m\}}|{\sf T}_{i}|\right)+2(|{\sf G}|-1)-|{\sf T}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>\log n\cdot\left(\sum_{i\in I\setminus\{m\}}\left(n_{i}\log n_{i}+n_{i}\right)+n_{m}\log n\right)+2(|{\sf G}|-1)-|{\sf T}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>\log n\cdot\left(\sum_{i\in I\setminus\{m\}}\left(n_{i}\log n\right)+n_{m}\log n\right)+2(|{\sf G}|-1)-|{\sf T}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>\log n\cdot\sum_{i\in I}\left(n_{i}\log n\right)+2(|{\sf G}|-1)-|{\sf T}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>n\log^{2}n+2(|{\sf G}|-1)-|{\sf T}|

using the fact that
\frac{n_{i}}{n}\leq\frac{1}{2}
for
i\neq m
(therefore
\log n-\log n_{i}\geq 1
).

Case
{\sf G}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\in\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\}
. Similar to the above case, with no merging. Thus this case can be performed in less time.

Case
{\sf G}=\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf G}^{\prime}
. Let
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{\sf G}^{\prime}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
. We have
|{\sf T}|\leq|{\sf T}^{\prime}|+1
. Checking for closedness was precomputed, thus this is a constant-time check and a recursion:
1+|{\sf G}^{\prime}|\log^{2}|{\sf G}^{\prime}|+2|{\sf G}^{\prime}|-|{\sf T}^{\prime}|\leq|{\sf G}|\log^{2}|{\sf G}|+2|{\sf G}|-|{\sf T}|
.

Case
{\sf G}=\mathbf{t},\mathtt{end}
. This takes constant time;
|{\sf G}|\log^{2}|{\sf G}|+2|{\sf G}|-|{\sf T}|=1
is positive.
∎

C.3.
Appendix for Complexity of Tirore
et al.
’s Binder-Agnostic Projection
(
Tirore et al., 2023
)

See
4.21

Proof.

Assume by
{\sf G}
is inductively
projectable by the full merging but not by plain merging.
We write
{\sf G}^{\prime}\in{\sf G}
if
{\sf G}^{\prime}
is syntactically subterm of
{\sf G}
.
Then there exits

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in{\sf G}
such that

{G_{k}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}\not\equiv{G_{h}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
with
k,h\in I
.
By definition of plain merging (Definition
4.14
),
if it is coinductively projectable with the plain merging,
\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}

for the branching types should be restricted as:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{k}:({\sf T}_{k}\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\sf T}_{k}^{\prime})\}_{\{}k\in I\}

with the rest of the rules of
\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}
in Definition
4.7
, which yields
\sqcap=\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{1.6146pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}
.
∎

We prove Theorem
4.22
,
recalling all definitions needed from
(
Tirore et al., 2023
)
.

Adapted to our syntax, the function
\mathsf{trans}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
is defined identically to inductive projection (Definition
4.5
) with the following merging function:
{\sf T}_{1}\sqcap{\sf T}_{2}={\sf T}_{1}
. Clearly,
\mathsf{trans}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
is not a sound projection function on its own, so we must rely on the checking of
{\sf G}\!\!\downharpoonleft_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}\mathsf{trans}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}({\sf G})
.

Definition C.9.

The graph of
({\sf G},{\sf T})
, with respect to
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
, has nodes
{\rm{Sub}}({\sf G})\times{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})
, with edges defined by the following rules:

{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\langle{S}\rangle;\>{\sf G}^{\prime}
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf T}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T}^{\prime}\vee{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf T}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T}^{\prime}
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\in\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\}
({\sf G},{\sf T})\rightarrow({\sf G}^{\prime},{\sf T}^{\prime})

{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf T}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in J}\vee{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf T}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in J}
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\in\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\}
k\in I\cap J

({\sf G},{\sf T})\rightarrow({\sf G}_{k},{\sf T}_{k})

{\sf G}\xrightarrow{}{\sf G}^{\prime}
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\langle{S}\rangle;\>{\sf G}^{\prime}\vee{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\notin\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\}
({\sf G},{\sf T})\rightarrow({\sf G}^{\prime},{\sf T})

Definition C.10.

For a graph
G=(V,E)
and predicate
P
,
\mathsf{sat}_{P}(\{\},v)
is a predicate that holds if
P
holds for all
w
such that
w
is reachable from
v
:

\mathsf{sat}_{P}(V,v)=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }v\in V\\
P(v)\wedge\bigwedge_{v\rightarrow u\in E}\mathsf{sat}_{P}(V,u)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Definition C.11.

The predicate
{\mathsf{ProjPred}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G},{\sf T})
is defined as follows:

{\mathsf{ProjPred}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G},{\sf T})=

\begin{cases}\mathsf{PL}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}(\mathsf{LG}({\sf G}))=\mathsf{LT}({\sf T})\wedge d_{g}({\sf G})=d_{l}({\sf T})&\text{if }\mathsf{PL}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}(\mathsf{LG}({\sf G}))\text{ is defined}\\
0<d_{g}({\sf G})&\text{if }\mathsf{partOf}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}({\sf G})\text{ and }\mathsf{guarded}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}({\sf G})\\
\mathsf{sat}_{\mathsf{UnravelPred}}(\{\},{\sf G})\wedge\neg\mathsf{partOf}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}({\sf G})\wedge{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf T}})=\mathtt{end}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

All terms on the right-hand side of
{\mathsf{ProjPred}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
, except for
\mathsf{sat}_{\mathsf{UnravelPred}}(\{\},{\sf G})
, are simple constant-time checks (possibly with some linear-time precomputation), which we omit. Also,
\mathsf{UnravelPred}
itself is a constant-time check. The following theorem is proven in
(
Tirore et al., 2023
)
:

Theorem C.12.

{{\sf G}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}={{\sf T}}
if
\mathsf{sat}_{{\mathsf{ProjPred}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}}(\{\},({\sf G},{\sf T}))
.

Therefore checking
\mathsf{sat}_{\mathsf{ProjPred}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{\},({\sf G},\mathsf{trans}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}({\sf G})))
is enough to decide projection.

C.3.1.
Complexity

\mathsf{trans}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
is easy to compute: merging takes constant time thus it can be computed in
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\right)
. Thus the main complexity of the algorithm comes from checking
\mathsf{sat}_{{\mathsf{ProjPred}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}}(\{\},({\sf G},{\sf T}))
. The two functions to consider are
\mathsf{sat}_{\mathsf{ProjPred}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
and
\mathsf{sat}_{\mathsf{UnravelPred}}
. We have the following observations:

Fact C.1.

For any graph
G=(V,E)
,
\mathsf{sat}_{P}(\{\},v)
can be computed in
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|V|\cdot T+|E|\right)
time, where
T
is the time taken to compute
P(v)
.

Proof.

Obvious; any graph search algorithm works.
∎

Fact C.2.

The size of the graph of
{\sf G}
is
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\right)
; the size of the graph of
({\sf G},{\sf T})
is
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\cdot|{\sf T}|\right)
.

Proof.

The number of nodes is bound by
|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf G})|
and
|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf G})|\cdot|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})|
respectively; the number of edges for each graph can be bounded using structural induction.
∎

Thus we can show that checking the projection takes quadratic time:

Lemma C.13.

Checking
\mathsf{sat}_{{\mathsf{ProjPred}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}}(\{\},({\sf G},{\sf T}))
can be done in
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\cdot|{\sf T}|\right)
time.

Proof.

First, computing
\mathsf{sat}_{\mathsf{UnravelPred}}(\{\},{\sf G}^{\prime})
for all
{\sf G}^{\prime}\in{\rm{Sub}}({\sf G})
takes
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\right)
time as
\mathsf{UnravelPred}
is a constant-time predicate (then using Observation
C.1
.)

After this precomputation,
{\mathsf{ProjPred}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
takes constant time to compute. So, checking
\mathsf{sat}_{{\mathsf{ProjPred}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}}(\{\},({\sf G},{\sf T}))
takes
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\rm{Sub}}({\sf G})\times{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})|\right)\leq\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\cdot|{\sf T}|\right)
time.
∎

Theorem C.14.

The projection in
(
Tirore et al., 2023
)
takes
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|^{2}\right)
time.

Proof.

We have shown above that checking
{\sf G}\!\!\downharpoonleft_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{\sf T}
takes
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\cdot|{\sf T}|\right)
time (Lemma
C.13
, Theorem
C.12
). Additionally, checking
\mathsf{trans}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
takes
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\right)
time. Using the fact that
|{\sf T}|\leq|{\sf G}|
(analogue of Lemma
C.6
for
\mathsf{trans}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
), we have the result.
∎

Theorem C.15.

The projection in
(
Tirore et al., 2023
)
takes
\Omega\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|^{2}\right)
time in the worst case.

Proof.

Define the (not necessarily closed) global type fragments:

\displaystyle{\sf G}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{t}

\displaystyle{\sf G}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{k}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;{\sf G}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{k-1}

\displaystyle(k>0)

\displaystyle{\sf T}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{t}

\displaystyle{\sf T}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{k}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}!\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;{\sf T}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{k-1}

\displaystyle(k>0)

We can construct a global type
{\sf G}
such that the the graph of
({\sf G},\mathsf{trans}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}({\sf G}))
has
\Theta\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|^{2}\right)
nodes:

\displaystyle{\sf G}

\displaystyle=\mu\mathbf{t}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{1}:\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf G}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{n},l_{2}:\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf G}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{n+1}\}

We have that
\mathsf{trans}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}{{\sf G}}=\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{n}
.
We have that
({\sf G},\mathsf{trans}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}({\sf G}))=({\sf G},\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{n})\rightarrow(\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf G}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{n+1},\mu\mathbf{t}.{\sf T}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{n})
.
From here, all nodes
({\sf G}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{i}\{\nicefrac{{{\sf G}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{n+1}}}{{\mathbf{t}}}\},{\sf T}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{j}\{\nicefrac{{{\sf T}^{\mathsf{rep}}_{n}}}{{\mathbf{t}}}\})
,
0\leq i\leq n,0\leq j\leq n-1
are reachable.
Using the fact that
|{\sf G}|=\Theta\negmedspace\left(n\right)
, the graph of
({\sf G},\mathsf{trans}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}({\sf G}))
has
\Theta\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|^{2}\right)
nodes, thus the algorithm takes
\Omega\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|^{2}\right)
time.
∎

C.4.
Appendix for Section
4.4

See
4.19

Proof.

Note that
{\sf G}
is
not
balanced if there exists a cycle from which some participant
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
is reachable,
but the cycle itself uses only nodes where
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
is not involved.
For each participant
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
, we can find all nodes that don’t involve
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
but
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
is reachable in
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\right)
time. We can then find cycles involving these nodes also in
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\right)
time. Thus, we can check if
{\sf G}
is balanced in
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|^{2}\right)
time, as there are
\mathcal{O}\negmedspace\left(|{\sf G}|\right)
participants.
∎

Example C.16 (Subset construction).

Consider the global type

\displaystyle\small{\sf G}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}:\{

\displaystyle l_{1}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{3}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;\mathtt{end}\},

\displaystyle l_{2}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{4}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{bool}}\rangle;\mathtt{end}\}\}

The subset construction for projection onto
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}
,
\mathbb{G}^{\mathsf{proj}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}}({\sf G})
, has
initial node

\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}}(\{{\sf G}\})=\{{\sf G},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{3}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;\mathtt{end}\},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{4}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{bool}}\rangle;\mathtt{end}\}\}
. Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}}(\{{\sf G}\})&\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}?\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle}&\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{3}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;\mathtt{end}\},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{4}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{bool}}\rangle;\mathtt{end}\}\}\ =\ \mathfrak{g}\\
\mathfrak{g}&\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}\&{l_{3}}}&\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;\mathtt{end}\}\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}!\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle}\{\mathtt{end}\}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}}\mathsf{{\small{Skip}}}\quad\text{and}\\
\mathfrak{g}&\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}\&{l_{4}}}&\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:\langle{\mathtt{bool}}\rangle;\mathtt{end}\}\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}!\langle{\mathtt{bool}}\rangle}\{\mathtt{end}\}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}}\mathsf{{\small{Skip}}}\end{array}

The corresponding local type is
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}?\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\&\!\{l_{3}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle;\mathtt{end},l_{4}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}!\langle{\mathtt{bool}}\rangle;\mathtt{end}\}
.

Lemma C.17 (Monotonicity and closure of
\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
).

(1)
\mathfrak{g}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
implies

\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\mathfrak{g})\subseteq\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})
; and
(2)
\mathfrak{g}\subseteq\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})
implies

\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{(\mathfrak{g})}\subseteq\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})}
.

Proof.

(1) By definition. (2) By (1) and
\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}))=\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})
.
∎

Lemma C.18.

Let
{\sf G}
be a balanced global type. If the projection graph
\mathbb{G}^{\mathsf{proj}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G})
is defined, then
\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{{\sf G}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}}\mathbb{G}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\})
for all nodes
\mathfrak{g}
in
\mathbb{G}^{\mathsf{proj}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G})
.

Proof.

We show that the set of equations
\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{{\sf G}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}}(\mathbb{G}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\}))
is consistent with the coinductive definition of merging.

Case
\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}}\mathsf{{\small{Skip}}}
. Then for all
{\sf G}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}
, we have
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\notin{\mathtt{pt}}\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\}
. Thus,
\mathbb{G}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\})=\mathtt{end}
, so the result follows.

Case
\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
. Then for all
{\sf G}_{i}\in\mathfrak{g}
involving
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
, we have
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}_{i}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}:\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf G}_{i}^{\prime}
,
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{i}^{\prime}\})
.
We have
\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{{\sf G}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}}\mathbb{G}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\})
. Thus,
\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{{\sf G}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}}\mathbb{G}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\})
is consistent with the definition.

Case
\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
. Symmetric.

Case
\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}\oplus{l_{j}}}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}_{j}
. Then for all
{\sf G}_{i}\in\mathfrak{g}
involving
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
,
we have
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}_{i}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{ij}\}_{j\in J}
.
For all
j\in J
,
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}_{j}=\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{ij}\})
.
We have
\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}_{j})={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{{\sf G}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}_{j}}\mathbb{G}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\})
.
Thus,
\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{{\sf G}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}}\mathbb{G}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\})
is consistent with the definition.

Case
\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}\&{l_{j}}}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}_{j}
. Then for all
{\sf G}_{i}\in\mathfrak{g}
involving
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
,
we have
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}_{i}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:\{l_{j}:{\sf G}_{ij}\}_{j\in J_{i}}
.
For all
j\in\bigcup_{i\in I}J_{i}
,

\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}_{j}=\bigcup_{i\in I,j\in J_{i}}\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{ij}\})
.
We have
\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}_{j})={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{{\sf G}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}_{j}}\mathbb{G}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\})
.
Thus,
\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{{\sf G}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}}\mathbb{G}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\})
is consistent with the definition.
∎

See
4.28

Proof.

We show that
\mathcal{R}=\{({\sf G}^{\prime},\mathbb{G}(\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\})))\ |\ {\sf G}^{\prime}\in{\rm{Sub}}({\sf G})\}
is a subset of the projection relation.

Consider some pair
({\sf G}^{\prime},\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g}))
, where
\mathfrak{g}=\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\})
.

Case
If
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\notin{\mathtt{pt}}\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\}
, then we have that
\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{end}}\mathsf{{\small{Skip}}}
. Thus by definition,
{\sf G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})
.

Otherwise, proceed by case analysis on the shape of
{\sf G}^{\prime}
.

Case
If
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}^{\prime}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}:\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf G}^{\prime\prime}
, then
\mathfrak{g}=\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\}\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle}\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime\prime}\})
.
We have that
{\sf G}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{R}\mathbb{G}(\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime\prime}\}))
so
{\sf G}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})
is consistent with the coinductive rules.

Case
If
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}^{\prime}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf G}^{\prime\prime}
, symmetric.

Case
If
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}^{\prime}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf G}^{\prime\prime}
, for
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\notin\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\}
,
then
{\sf G}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{R}\mathbb{G}(\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime\prime}\}))
. Note that
\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime\prime}\})\cup{\sf G}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{g}
,
but
{\sf G}^{\prime}
does not involve
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
so
\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})=\mathbb{G}(\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}^{\prime\prime}\}))
.
Therefore
{\sf G}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})
is consistent with the coinductive rules.

Case
If
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}^{\prime}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, then,
for all
i\in I
,

\mathfrak{g}=\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\}\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}\oplus{l_{i}}}\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{i}\})
.
We have that
{\sf G}_{i}\mathcal{R}\mathbb{G}(\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{i}\}))
so
{\sf G}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})
is consistent with the coinductive rules.

Case
If
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}^{\prime}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, then,
for all
i\in I
,

\mathfrak{g}=\{{\sf G}^{\prime}\}\xrightarrow{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}\&{l_{i}}}\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{i}\})
.
We have that
{\sf G}_{i}\mathcal{R}\mathbb{G}(\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{i}\}))
so
{\sf G}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})
is consistent with the coinductive rules.

Case
If
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf G}^{\prime}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}:\{l_{i}:{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
for
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\notin\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}\}
,
then,
{\sf G}_{i}\mathcal{R}\mathbb{G}(\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{i}\}))
for all
i\in I
.
Note that
\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{i}\})\cup{\sf G}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{g}
,
but
{\sf G}^{\prime}
does not involve
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
so
\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})=\mathbb{G}(\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{i}\}))={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I}\mathbb{G}(\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}})(\{{\sf G}_{i}\})
,
by Lemma
C.18
.
Therefore
{\sf G}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}\mathbb{G}(\mathfrak{g})
is consistent with the coinductive rules.

Thus,
{\sf G}\upharpoonright_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}\mathbb{G}(\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}\}))
, as required.
∎

Lemma C.19.

Let
{\sf G}
be a balanced global type such
that
{\sf G}\upharpoonright_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{\sf T}
and
\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G})=\{{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
.
Then there exists
{\sf G}_{i}\upharpoonright_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{\sf T}_{i}
such that
(1)
{\sf T}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I}{\sf T}_{i}
; and
(2)
{\sf T}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{k\in K}{\sf T}_{k}
where

K=\{i\ |\ {\mathsf{inv}}({{\sf G}_{i}},{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}),\ i\in I\}
.

Proof.

By the definition of a balanced property and the definition of
{\sf G}\upharpoonright_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{\sf T}
,
all global types in
\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G})
that do not involve
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
has a projection which is the merging of
other projections of
\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G})
. Furthermore, the dependencies of the projections do not have a cycle.
Thus, we may find the projections of the global types in
\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G})
that involve
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
, and merge
them together to obtain all other projections. Thus (2) follows.
(1) follows from the fact that merging is idempotent.
∎

See
4.29

Proof.

For contradiction, assume that
{\sf G}\upharpoonright_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{\sf T}
but some local type
\mathfrak{g}\in\mathbb{G}^{\mathsf{proj}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G})
is invalid
(i.e. its outgoing edges do not match any of the rules in the subset construction).
Then, there exists a path in
\mathbb{G}^{\mathsf{proj}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G})
from
\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G})
to
\mathfrak{g}
.

Without loss of generality
\mathfrak{g}
is the first invalid node along the path.
We show by induction that for all nodes
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}=\{{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
along the path,
there exists
{\sf G}_{i}\upharpoonright_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{\sf T}_{i}
and
{\sf T}^{\prime}
such that
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I}{\sf T}_{i}
.

First, the initial node
\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}({\sf G})
contains
{\sf G}
, and
{\sf G}\upharpoonright_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{\sf T}
by assumption.

Now, consider some edge
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\prime}
, where
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}=\{{\sf G}_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
,
and assume there exists
{\sf G}_{i}^{\prime}\upharpoonright_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime}
and
{\sf T}^{\prime}\in{\rm{Sub}}({\sf T})
such that
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I}{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime}
.

We analyse the possible cases for
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}
. Let
I^{*}=\{i\in I\>|\>{\sf G}_{i}^{\prime}\text{ involves }{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\}
.

Case
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}=\mathsf{end}
. Then,
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\prime}=\mathsf{{\small{Skip}}}
. Contradiction, as
\mathsf{{\small{Skip}}}
is always valid.

Case
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}={{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle
. Then,
{\sf G}_{i}^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}:\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf G}_{i}^{\prime\prime}
for all
i\in I^{*}

For all such
i
,
{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime\prime}
. Thus,
{\sf G}_{i}^{\prime\prime}\upharpoonright_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime\prime}
,
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\prime}=\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}_{i\in I^{*}})
.
By Lemma
C.19
,
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I^{*}}{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime}
.
Thus,
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T}^{\prime\prime}
for
{\sf T}^{\prime\prime}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I^{*}}{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime\prime}
.
Using Lemma
C.19
again, we get the desired result.

Case
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}={{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle
. Symmetric.

Case
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}={{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}\oplus{l_{k}}
. Then,
{\sf G}_{i}^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}:\{l_{j}:{\sf G}_{ij}\}_{j\in J}
for all
i\in I^{*}
.
For all such
i
,
{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{k}\}{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime\prime}
,
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\prime}=\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{ik}\}_{i\in I^{*}})
.
By Lemma
C.19
,
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I^{*}}{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime}
.
Thus,
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{k}\}{\sf T}^{\prime\prime}
for
{\sf T}^{\prime\prime}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I^{*}}{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime\prime}
.
Using Lemma
C.19
again, we get the desired result.

Case
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}={{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}\&{l_{k}}
. Then,
{\sf G}_{i}^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}:\{l_{j}:{\sf G}_{ij}\}_{j\in J_{i}}
for all
i\in I^{*}
.
For all such
i
,
{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{k}\}{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime\prime}
,
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\prime}=\mathscr{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}(\{{\sf G}_{ik}\}_{k\in J_{i},i\in I^{*}})
.
By Lemma
C.19
,
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{k\in J_{i},i\in I^{*}}{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime}
.
Thus,
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{k}\}{\sf T}^{\prime\prime}
for
{\sf T}^{\prime\prime}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{k\in J_{i},i\in I^{*}}{\sf T}_{i}^{\prime\prime}
.
Using Lemma
C.19
again, we get the desired result.

Therefore, for
\mathfrak{g}=\{{\sf G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, there exists
{\sf G}_{i}\upharpoonright_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}{\sf T}_{i}
and
{\sf T}^{\prime}
such that
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{{\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 5.76001pt\hskip-5.76001pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\sqcap$}$}\hskip-5.76001pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{3.63683pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\text{\tiny$\star$}$}$}}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 5.76001pt}}}}}_{i\in I}{\sf T}_{i}
.
But there are incompatible outgoing edges from
\mathfrak{g}
, so the projections of
{\sf G}_{i}
must have unmergeable first messages.
∎

Appendix D
Appendix for Minimum Typing System

D.1.
Typing Multiparty Sessions

We recall the typing system of expressions from
(
Ghilezan et al., 2019
)
.

Definition D.1.

The typing rules for expressions are given below:

[
T-SortVar
]

x:S\in\Gamma
\Gamma\vdash x:S

[
T-True
]

\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{true}:\mathtt{bool}

[
T-False
]

\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{false}:\mathtt{bool}

[
T-Nat
]

\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{n}:\mathtt{nat}

[
T-Int
]

\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{i}:\mathtt{int}

[
T-Neg
]

\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{e}:\mathtt{int}
\Gamma\vdash{\tt neg}(\mathsf{e}):\mathtt{int}

[
T-Not
]

\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{e}:\mathtt{bool}
\Gamma\vdash\neg\mathsf{e}:\mathtt{bool}

[
T-Or
]

\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{e}_{1}:\mathtt{bool}
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{e}_{2}:\mathtt{bool}
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{e}_{1}\vee\mathsf{e}_{2}:\mathtt{bool}

[
T-Nondet
]

\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{e}_{1}:S
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{e}_{2}:S
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{e}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{e}_{2}:S

[
T-Add
]

\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{e}_{1}:\mathtt{int}
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{e}_{2}:\mathtt{int}
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{e}_{1}+\mathsf{e}_{2}:\mathtt{int}

D.2.
Constraint Inference Rules for Expressions

We explain the rules for the expressions in Figure
6
.

[
C-SortVar
]
states that a sort variable
x
must have a matching sort with the typing context.
Rules
[
C-True
]
,
[
C-False
]
,
[
C-Nat
]
, and
[
C-Int
]
state that the expressions
\mathsf{true}
,
\mathsf{false}
,
\mathsf{n}
, and
\mathsf{i}
must have the corresponding sorts.
Rule
[
C-Neg
]
states that the expression
{\tt neg}(\mathsf{e})
and
\mathsf{e}
must both have sort
\mathtt{int}
.
Rule
[
C-Not
]
states that the expression
\neg\mathsf{e}
and
\mathsf{e}
must both have sort
\mathtt{bool}
.
Similarly,
[
C-Or
]
states that all relevant expressions must have sort
\mathtt{bool}
.
Rule
[
C-Nondet
]
states that the expressions
\mathsf{e}_{1}
,
\mathsf{e}_{2}
and
\mathsf{e}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{e}_{2}
must have the same sort.
Rule
[
C-Add
]
states that the expressions
\mathsf{e}_{1}
,
\mathsf{e}_{2}
and
\mathsf{e}_{1}+\mathsf{e}_{2}
must have sort
\mathtt{int}
.

D.3.
Proofs for Soundness and Completeness of Constraint Derivations

See
5.8

Proof.

We prove by induction on the proof trees of
\Gamma\vdash P:\xi\mid_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{C}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash P:\xi^{\prime}\mid_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.

Case
[
C-End
]
. Then
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=\emptyset
. Take
\sigma=\emptyset
,
\pi=\emptyset
.

Case
[
C-Bra
]
. Then
P={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\triangleright\{l_{i}:P_{i}\}_{i\in I}
.

Then we have proof trees for
\Gamma\vdash P_{i}:\xi_{i}\mid_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}\mathcal{C}_{i}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash P_{i}:\xi_{i}^{\prime}\mid_{\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\prime}}\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime}
, for all
i\in I
. By the inductive hypothesis, there exist one-to-one functions
\sigma_{i},\pi_{i}
such that
\mathcal{C}_{i}\pi_{i}\sigma_{i}=\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime}
. Let
\sigma=\bigcup_{i\in I}\sigma_{i}\cup\{\xi\mapsto\xi^{\prime}\}
and
\pi=\bigcup_{i\in I}\pi_{i}
. Then, as
\mathcal{X}_{i},\xi,\xi^{\prime}
are disjoint,
\sigma
and
\pi
are one-to-one. Furthermore,

\mathcal{C}\pi\sigma=\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.

Other cases: Similar.
∎

Lemma D.2.

Let
\Gamma\vdash P:{\sf T}
be derivable. Then there exists a derivation of
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash P:\xi\mid_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{C}
such that
\operatorname{dom}({\Gamma})=\operatorname{dom}({\Gamma^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

By matching each typing rule with the corresponding constraint rule.
∎

See
5.10

Proof.

We show that if
\Gamma\vdash P:\xi\mid_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{C}
