{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{m}}?\langle{\mathtt{int}}\rangle
{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{m}}\oplus{\mathsf{ff}}

Figure 13.

Graphs of the participants in Figure
12
.
Note that the graphs for
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}
differ only on the edges incident to
T_{r}
and
F_{r}
.

Notation.

We use the following notation to refer to the reachable contexts of the participants. Let
({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{s}}}}:{\sf T}^{*}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{s}}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{1}:{\sf T}^{*}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{1}},\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{n}:{\sf T}^{*}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{n}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}:{\sf T}^{*}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}},\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{m+1}:{\sf T}^{*}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{m+1}})
refer to the context
\prod_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\in\mathbb{P}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:{\sf T}^{*}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}}
(this is the same order as the chain of communications: each type communicates with only the types directly left and right of it). We may omit some or all of the prefixes
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:
if it is clear from the context.

For an assignment of truth values
\mathcal{A}:\{v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
with
k\leq n
, define
V^{i}=T
if
\mathcal{A}(v_{i})=1
, and
V^{i}=F
otherwise. Define
({\sf T}^{*}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{s}}}}},\mathcal{A},{\sf T}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{k+1}},\dots,{\sf T}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{n}})=({\sf T}^{*}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{s}}}}},V^{1},\dots,V^{k},{\sf T}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{k+1}},\dots,{\sf T}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{n}})
. Define
[\mathcal{A}]=(W,\mathcal{A},I_{1},I,\dots,I)
.
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}=(I,\dots,I)
is the starting context.

E.5.1.
Reduction Behaviour of the Composition

Definition E.4 (Deterministic, safe reduction paths).

The reduction
\Delta\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
is
deterministic

if
\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{2}

imply
\Delta_{1}=\Delta_{2}=\Delta^{\prime}
. We write

\Delta\hookrightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
if

\Delta\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
is deterministic.
For safe states
\Delta
, define
\Delta\Rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
if
\Delta\hookrightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
.

We write
\hookrightarrow^{\ast}
for the reflexive and transitive closure of
\hookrightarrow
. We write
\Delta\Rightarrow^{\ast}\Delta^{\prime}
if
\Delta
is a safe state and
\Delta^{\prime}
can be reached from
\Delta
by a sequence of
\Rightarrow
reductions.

We will now describe the behaviour of the context in Figure
12
, primarily to show that it has a unique and safe reduction path that calculates the truth value of the QBF.

The following lemma states that processes handling clauses can “query” the truth value of variables by selecting the label
\mathsf{query}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}}
and will get the truth value in the form of a selection of
\mathsf{yes}
or
\mathsf{no}
.

Lemma E.5 (Propagation of
\mathsf{query}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}}
).

Let
\Delta=(W,\mathcal{A},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}:R,\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:{\sf T},I,\dots,I)
for some type
{\sf T}
. Let
{\sf T}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}\!\oplus\!\{\mathsf{query}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}}:{\sf T}^{\prime}\}
, such that
{\sf T}^{\prime}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}\&\!\{\mathsf{yes}:{\sf T}_{\mathsf{yes}},\mathsf{no}:{\sf T}_{\mathsf{no}}\}
. Then:

•

If
\mathcal{A}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i})=1
, then
\Delta\Rightarrow^{\ast}(W,\mathcal{A},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}:R,\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}:R,{\sf T}_{\mathsf{yes}},I,\dots,I)
.

•

If
\mathcal{A}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i})=0
, then
\Delta\Rightarrow^{\ast}(W,\mathcal{A},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}:R,\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}:R,{\sf T}_{\mathsf{no}},I,\dots,I)
.

Proof.

We focus on the case where
\mathcal{A}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i})=1
; the other case is symmetric. Defining
(V^{j})^{{\mathtt{pt}}\{t\}}_{11}=T^{{\mathtt{pt}}\{t\}}_{11}
if
V^{j}=T
, and
(V^{j})^{{\mathtt{pt}}\{t\}}_{11}=F^{{\mathtt{pt}}\{t\}}_{11}
if
V^{j}=F
,
etc.
, observe that:

\begin{array}[]{rll}\small\Delta\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{2}:R,\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-3}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-2}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}:R_{1}^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{2}:R,\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-3}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-2}:R_{1}^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}:R_{2},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
&\dots\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}:R_{1}^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{2}:R_{2},\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-3}:R_{2},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-2}:R_{2},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}:R_{2},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
=&(W,V^{1},\dots,V^{i-1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}:T,V^{i+1},V^{i+2},\dots,V^{n-1},V^{n},R_{1}^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}},R_{2},\dots,R_{2},{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,V^{1},\dots,V^{i-1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}:T,V^{i+1},V^{i+2},\dots,V^{n-1},(V^{n})^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}}_{11},R_{2},R_{2},\dots,R_{2},{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,V^{1},\dots,V^{i-1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}:T,V^{i+1},V^{i+2},\dots,(V^{n-1})^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}}_{11},V^{n}_{12},R_{2},R_{2},\dots,R_{2},{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
&\dots\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{lll}\Rightarrow&(W,V^{1},\dots,V^{i-1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}:T,(V^{i+1})^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}}_{11},V^{i+2}_{12},\dots,V^{n-1}_{12},V^{n}_{12},R_{2},R_{2},\dots,R_{2},{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,V^{1},\dots,V^{i-1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}:T_{21},V^{i+1}_{12},V^{i+2}_{12},\dots,V^{n-1}_{12},V^{n}_{12},R_{2},R_{2},\dots,R_{2},{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,V^{1},\dots,V^{i-1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}:T,V^{i+1}_{13},V^{i+2}_{12},\dots,V^{n-1}_{12},V^{n}_{12},R_{2},R_{2},\dots,R_{2},{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,V^{1},\dots,V^{i-1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}:T,V^{i+1},V^{i+2}_{13},\dots,V^{n-1}_{12},V^{n}_{12},R_{2},R_{2},\dots,R_{2},{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
&\dots\\
\Rightarrow&(W,V^{1},\dots,V^{i-1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}:T,V^{i+1},V^{i+2},\dots,V^{n-1},V^{n}_{13},R_{2},R_{2},\dots,R_{2},{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,V^{1},\dots,V^{i-1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}:T,V^{i+1},V^{i+2},\dots,V^{n-1},V^{n},R_{3},R_{2},\dots,R_{2},{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
=&(W,\mathcal{A},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}:R_{3},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{2}:R_{2},\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-2}:R_{2},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}:R_{2},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{2}:R_{3},\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-2}:R_{2},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}:R_{2},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
&\dots\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{2}:R,\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-2}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}:R_{3},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:{\sf T}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{1}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{2}:R,\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-2}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k-1}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:{\sf T}_{\mathsf{yes}},I,\dots,I)\end{array}

Intuitively, the message
\mathsf{query}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}}
is being propagated through the chain of participants, starting at
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}
and ending at
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}
, and then the “return value” of
\mathsf{yes}
is propagated backwards through the same chain. The states of the participants store the necessary information to uniquely specify this reduction path.
∎

The next lemma details how
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{i}
finds the truth value of the conjunction of all clauses starting from
\mathcal{C}_{i}
.
As the participants
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{i}
use the
\mathsf{query}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}}
labels to find the truth values of their variables,
we use Lemma
E.5
to specify the results of this behaviour.
As one might expect,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{i}
enters state
T_{r}
, ready to send a
\mathsf{tt}
to
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{i-1}

if the conjunction is true, otherwise it will enter state
F_{r}
and instead send
\mathsf{ff}
.

Lemma E.6 (Reduction of
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{i}
).

For
\mathcal{A}:\{v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
:

•

(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:I_{1},I,\dots,I)\Rightarrow^{\ast}(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:T_{r},I,\dots,I)
if
\mathcal{A}\models\bigwedge_{i=k}^{m}C_{i}
.

•

(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:I_{1},I,\dots,I)\Rightarrow^{\ast}(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:F_{r},I,\dots,I)
if
\mathcal{A}\not\models\bigwedge_{i=k}^{m}C_{i}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k}=\bigwedge_{i=k}^{m}C_{i}
. The proof is by induction on
k
. For
k=m
,
F
and
I_{1}
are the same state, so the result follows immediately.
For
k<m
, we have two cases to consider.

Case
\mathcal{A}\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k}
. We have that
\mathcal{A}\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k+1}
and at least one of
\mathcal{A}(L_{k1})
,
\mathcal{A}(L_{k2})
and
\mathcal{A}(L_{k3})
is
1
.

If
\mathcal{A}(L_{k1})=1
:

\begin{array}[]{lll}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:I_{1},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k+1}:I_{1},I\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k+1}:T_{r},I,\dots,I)&\text{by inductive hypothesis}\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:Q_{1}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:T_{r},I,\dots,I)&\text{by Lemma~\ref{thm:pspace_query_reduction}}\end{array}

If
\mathcal{A}(L_{k1})=0
and
\mathcal{A}(L_{k2})=1
:

\begin{array}[]{lll}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:I_{1},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k+1}:I_{1},I\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k+1}:T_{r},I,\dots,I)&\text{by inductive hypothesis}\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:Q_{1}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:Q_{2}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)&\text{by Lemma~\ref{thm:pspace_query_reduction}}\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:T_{r},I,\dots,I)&\text{by Lemma~\ref{thm:pspace_query_reduction}}\\
\end{array}

If
\mathcal{A}(L_{k1})=0
and
\mathcal{A}(L_{k2})=0
and
\mathcal{A}(L_{k3})=1
:

\begin{array}[]{lll}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:I_{1},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k+1}:I_{1},I\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k+1}:T_{r},I,\dots,I)&\text{by inductive hypothesis}\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:Q_{1}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:Q_{2}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)&\text{by Lemma~\ref{thm:pspace_query_reduction}}\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:Q_{3}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)&\text{by Lemma~\ref{thm:pspace_query_reduction}}\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:T_{r},I,\dots,I)&\text{by Lemma~\ref{thm:pspace_query_reduction}}\\
\end{array}

Case
\mathcal{A}\not\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k}
. We have that
\mathcal{A}\not\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k+1}
or
\mathcal{A}(L_{k1})=\mathcal{A}(L_{k2})=\mathcal{A}(L_{k3})=0
.

If
\mathcal{A}\not\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k+1}
:

\begin{array}[]{lll}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:I_{1},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k+1}:I_{1},I\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k+1}:F_{r},I,\dots,I)&\text{by inductive hypothesis}\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:F_{r},I,\dots,I)\\
\end{array}

If
\mathcal{A}\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k+1}
and
\mathcal{A}(L_{k1})=\mathcal{A}(L_{k2})=\mathcal{A}(L_{k3})=0
:

\begin{array}[]{lll}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:I_{1},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k+1}:I_{1},I\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k+1}:T_{r},I,\dots,I)&\text{by inductive hypothesis}\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:Q_{1}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:Q_{2}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)&\text{by Lemma~\ref{thm:pspace_query_reduction}}\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:Q_{3}^{\prime},I,\dots,I)&\text{by Lemma~\ref{thm:pspace_query_reduction}}\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A},R,\dots,R,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{k}:F_{r},I,\dots,I)&\text{by Lemma~\ref{thm:pspace_query_reduction}}\\
\end{array}

Each case yields the desired result, so the lemma holds.
∎

Next, we show the behaviour of
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}
, which behaves similarly to
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{i}
, but instead handles the quantified variables.

Lemma E.7 (Reduction of
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{i}
).

For
\mathcal{A}:\{v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
:

•

[\mathcal{A}]\Rightarrow^{\ast}(W,\mathcal{A},T_{r},I,\dots,I)
if
\mathcal{A}\models\mathcal{Q}_{k+1}v_{k+1}\dots\mathcal{Q}_{n}v_{n}\mathcal{F^{\prime}}
.

•

[\mathcal{A}]\Rightarrow^{\ast}(W,\mathcal{A},F_{r},I,\dots,I)
if
\mathcal{A}\not\models\mathcal{Q}_{k+1}v_{k+1}\dots\mathcal{Q}_{n}v_{n}\mathcal{F^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k}=\mathcal{Q}_{k+1}v_{k+1}\dots\mathcal{Q}_{n}v_{n}\mathcal{F^{\prime}}
.
The proof is by induction on
k
. For
k=0
, use Lemma
E.6
.
For
k>0
, we consider the case
\mathcal{Q}_{k+1}=\forall
; the
\exists
case is similar.

If
\mathcal{A}\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k}
,
we have
\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 0]}\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k}

and
\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 1]}\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k}
.

\begin{array}[]{lll}[\mathcal{A}]\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},F,I_{1},\dots,I)=[\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 0]}]\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 0]},T_{r},I,\dots,I)=(W,\mathcal{A},F,T_{r},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},I_{2},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},T,I_{1},\dots,I)=[\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 1]}]\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 1]},T_{r},I,\dots,I)=(W,\mathcal{A},T,T_{r},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},T_{r},I,\dots,I)\\
\end{array}

If
\mathcal{A}\not\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k}
, then there are two cases: first, (i)
\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 0]}\not\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k}
:

\begin{array}[]{lll}[\mathcal{A}]\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},F,I_{1},\dots,I)=[\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 0]}]\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 0]},F_{r},I,\dots,I)=(W,\mathcal{A},F,F_{r},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},F_{r},I,\dots,I)\\
\end{array}

Second, (ii)
\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 0]}\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k}
and
\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 1]}\not\models\mathcal{F^{\prime}}_{k}
:

\begin{array}[]{lll}[\mathcal{A}]\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},F,I_{1},\dots,I)=[\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 0]}]\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 0]},T_{r},I,\dots,I)=(W,\mathcal{A},F,T_{r},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},I_{2},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},T,I_{1},\dots,I)=[\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 1]}]\\
\Rightarrow^{\ast}&(W,\mathcal{A}_{[v_{k+1}\mapsto 1]},F_{r},I,\dots,I)=(W,\mathcal{A},T,F_{r},I,\dots,I)\\
\Rightarrow&(W,\mathcal{A},F_{r},I,\dots,I)\\
\end{array}

Each
\Rightarrow
can be checked directly, and each
\Rightarrow^{\ast}
is from the inductive hypothesis.
Therefore, the lemma follows by induction.
∎

Theorem E.8.

Recall that

[\emptyset]=({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{s}}}}{:}W,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{1}{:}I_{1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{2}{:}I,\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{m+1}{:}I)
.
Then:
(1)

\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}\Rightarrow[\emptyset]
;

(2)

If
\models\mathcal{F}
, then
[\emptyset]\Rightarrow^{\ast}\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}
;
and
(3)
If
\not\models\mathcal{F}
, then
[\emptyset]\Rightarrow^{\ast}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{s}}}}{:}\mathsf{T}_{\rm{bad}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{1}{:}I,\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{r}}}}_{m+1}{:}I)
.

Proof.

The first statement is true by inspection of the transition system. The latter two statements are true by Lemma
E.7
with
k=0
.
∎

We can now use the above lemmas to prove
PSPACE
-hardness for checking safety, liveness and deadlock-freedom.

E.6.
PSPACE
-Hardness of Safety

Lemma E.9.

If
\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{1}\Rightarrow^{\ast}\Delta

and
\Delta\rightarrow^{*}\Delta^{\prime}

and
\Delta
is a safe state
and
\Delta^{\prime}
is not a safe state
then
\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{2}\rightarrow^{*}\Delta^{\prime}

for some
\Delta_{2}\neq\Delta_{1}
.

Proof.

By contradiction. Assume that
\Delta
,
\Delta_{1}
,
\Delta^{\prime}
are as described,
but there is no
\Delta_{2}\neq\Delta_{1}
such that
\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{2}\rightarrow^{*}\Delta^{\prime}
.
Fix the shortest reduction sequence
\Delta=\Delta^{0}\rightarrow\Delta^{1}\dots\rightarrow\Delta^{n}=\Delta^{\prime}
.
As
\Delta
is a safe state and
\Delta^{\prime}
is not a safe state,
\Delta\neq\Delta^{\prime}
and so
n>0
.
From our contradiction assumption,
\Delta^{1}=\Delta_{1}
.
As
\Delta_{1}\Rightarrow^{\ast}\Delta
, fix
\Delta^{{\dagger}}_{i}
such that
\Delta^{{\dagger}}_{1}\Rightarrow\Delta^{{\dagger}}_{2}\Rightarrow\dots\Rightarrow\Delta^{{\dagger}}_{m}=\Delta
.
By the definition of
\Rightarrow
(Definition
E.4
),
\Delta^{{\dagger}}_{i}=\Delta^{i}
and
\Delta^{i}
is a safe state for all
i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
,
so
\Delta^{m}=\Delta
.
But then
\Delta=\Delta^{m}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow\Delta^{n}=\Delta^{\prime}
is a shorter reduction sequence, contradicting our assumption of minimality.
∎

Theorem E.10.

Checking for safety is
PSPACE
-hard.

Proof.

Set
\mathsf{T}_{\rm{bad}}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{1}\&\!\{\mathsf{unsafe}:\mathtt{end}\}
.

•

If
\models\mathcal{F}
, then by Theorem
E.8
,
[\emptyset]\Rightarrow^{\ast}\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}
. Assume for contradiction that
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
for some
\Delta^{\prime}
that is not a safe state. We have
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}\rightarrow[\emptyset]
, so by Lemma
E.9
,
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}\rightarrow\Delta_{2}
for some
\Delta_{2}\neq[\emptyset]
. But no such
\Delta_{2}
exists, so
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}
is safe by Lemma
6.4
.

•

If
\not\models\mathcal{F}
, then by Theorem
E.8
,
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}\rightarrow[\emptyset]\Rightarrow^{\ast}(\mathsf{T}_{\rm{bad}},I,\dots,I)
. But
(\mathsf{T}_{\rm{bad}},I,\dots,I)\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{s}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{1}\&\mathsf{unsafe}}
and
(\mathsf{T}_{\rm{bad}},I,\dots,I)\not\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}_{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{s}}}}:\mathsf{unsafe}}
, so it is not a safe state. Thus
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}
is not safe.

Therefore,
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}
is safe if and only if
\models\mathcal{F}
. As QBF is
PSPACE
-complete, we conclude that checking for safety is
PSPACE
-hard.
∎

E.7.
PSPACE
-Hardness of Deadlock-Freedom

The proof that deadlock-freedom is
PSPACE
-hard is similar to the above proof for safety, as unique reduction paths also guarantee deadlock-freedom. First, we prove the analogue of Lemma
E.9
for deadlock-freedom.

Lemma E.11.

If
\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{1}\hookrightarrow^{\ast}\Delta

and
\Delta\rightarrow^{*}\Delta^{\prime}\not\rightarrow

then
\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{2}\rightarrow^{*}\Delta^{\prime}

for some
\Delta_{2}\neq\Delta_{1}
.

Proof.

By contradiction. Assume that
\Delta
,
\Delta_{1}
,
\Delta^{\prime}
are as described,
but there is no
\Delta_{2}\neq\Delta_{1}
such that
\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{2}\rightarrow^{*}\Delta^{\prime}
.
Fix the shortest reduction sequence
\Delta=\Delta^{0}\rightarrow\Delta^{1}\dots\rightarrow\Delta^{n}=\Delta^{\prime}
.
As
\Delta
is a safe state and
\Delta^{\prime}
is not a safe state,
\Delta\neq\Delta^{\prime}
and so
n>0
.
From our contradiction assumption,
\Delta^{1}=\Delta_{1}
.
As
\Delta_{1}\hookrightarrow^{\ast}\Delta
, fix
\Delta^{{\dagger}}_{i}
such that
\Delta^{{\dagger}}_{1}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{{\dagger}}_{2}\hookrightarrow\dots\hookrightarrow\Delta^{{\dagger}}_{m}=\Delta
.
By the definition of
\hookrightarrow
(Definition
E.4
),
\Delta^{{\dagger}}_{i}=\Delta^{i}
and
\Delta^{i}\rightarrow
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
,
so
\Delta^{m}=\Delta
.
But then
\Delta=\Delta^{m}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow\Delta^{n}=\Delta^{\prime}
is a shorter reduction sequence, contradicting our assumption of minimality.
∎

Theorem E.12.

Checking for deadlock-freedom is
PSPACE
-hard.

Proof.

Set
\mathsf{T}_{\rm{bad}}=\mathtt{end}
.

•

If
\models\mathcal{F}
, then by Theorem
E.8
,
[\emptyset]\Rightarrow^{\ast}\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}
. Assume for contradiction that
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
for some
\Delta^{\prime}\not\rightarrow
. We have
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}\rightarrow[\emptyset]
, so by Lemma
E.9
,
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}\rightarrow\Delta_{2}
for some
\Delta_{2}\neq[\emptyset]
. But no such
\Delta_{2}
exists, so
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}
is deadlock-free by Lemma
E.11
.

•

If
\not\models\mathcal{F}
, then by Theorem
E.8
,
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}\rightarrow[\emptyset]\Rightarrow^{\ast}(\mathsf{T}_{\rm{bad}},I,\dots,I)
. But
(\mathsf{T}_{\rm{bad}},I,\dots,I)\not\rightarrow
, so
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}
is not deadlock-free (because
{\mathsf{unfold}}({I})\neq\mathtt{end}
).

Therefore,
\Delta_{\mathsf{init}}
is deadlock-free if and only if
\models\mathcal{F}
. As QBF is
PSPACE
-complete, we conclude that checking for deadlock-freedom is
PSPACE
-hard.
∎

E.8.
PSPACE
-Hardness of Liveness

First, we prove some properties about liveness that will be useful in our proof.

Lemma E.13.

If
\Delta
is live then
\Delta
is deadlock-free.

Proof.

By
(
Yoshida and Hou, 2024
, Theorem 4)
.
∎

Definition E.14 (Participants of Reductions).

Define the participants of a reduction:
{\mathtt{pt}}\{\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}}\}=\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\}
and
{\mathtt{pt}}\{\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}:l}\}=\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\}
.

Lemma E.15.

Let
(\Delta_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be an infinite path such that
\Delta_{i}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}_{i}}\Delta_{i+1}
for all
i
.
If
\bigcup_{i\geq j}{\mathtt{pt}}\{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}_{i}\}={\mathtt{pt}}\{\Delta\}
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
, then
(\Delta_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is a live path.

Proof.

Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
, and let
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\in{\mathtt{pt}}\{\Delta\}
. Let
\Delta={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:{\sf T},\Delta^{\prime}
.
By assumption there exists
j\geq i
such that
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}\in{\mathtt{pt}}\{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}_{j}\}
.
Choose the smallest such
j
.
Then, no transition before
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}_{j}
affects
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
, so
\Delta_{j}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}})={\sf T}
.
We consider the syntax of
{\mathsf{unfold}}({{\sf T}})
:

Case
{\sf T}=\mathtt{end}
. Then there are no transitions involving
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
.

Case
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}?\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T}^{\prime}
. By considering the rules of Definition
6.2
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}_{j}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}
, as desired.

Case
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}!\langle{S}\rangle;{\sf T}^{\prime}
. Similar to the above:
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}_{j}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}
.

Case
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\!\oplus\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
. We conclude that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}_{j}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}:l_{k}
for some
k\in I
.

Case
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}\&\!\{l_{i}:{\sf T}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
. We conclude that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}_{j}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{q}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf{p}}}}:l_{k}
for some
k\in I
.

All transitions in the definition of live path fall into one of the above cases, so the path is live.
∎

Theorem E.16.

Checking for liveness is
PSPACE
-hard.

Proof.

We use the same construction as the deadlock-freedom reduction (Theorem
E.12
): set
\mathsf{T}_{\rm{bad}}=\mathtt{end}
.

•

If
\models\mathcal{F}
, then by the same argument as Theorem
E.12
,
\Delta\hookrightarrow[\emptyset]\hookrightarrow^{\ast}\Delta
.
Thus there is a unique infinite path
\Delta\hookrightarrow^{\omega}
.
We will show that this path is live.

Furthermore, the reduction sequence
\Delta\rightarrow(W,I_{1},I,\dots,I)\hookrightarrow\dots\hookrightarrow(W,F,\dots,F,R,\dots,R)
involves all participants in at least one transition.
As
\Delta_{j}\hookrightarrow^{\ast}\Delta
for all
\Delta_{j}
, this reduction sequence occurs after
\Delta_{j}
, therefore
\bigcup_{i\geq j}{\mathtt{pt}}\{{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\ell}}_{i}\}={\mathtt{pt}}\{\Delta\}
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
.
We can then apply Lemma
E.15
to conclude that
\Delta\hookrightarrow^{\omega}
is a live path.
As all reachable contexts have outgoing transitions (due to the path being deterministic), there are no finite fair paths.
Furthermore, we have shown that the only infinite path is live, so all fair paths are live.
We conclude that
\Delta
is live.

•

If
\not\models\mathcal{F}
, then by the proof of Theorem
E.12
,
\Delta
is not deadlock-free. By Lemma
E.13
,
\Delta
is not live.

Therefore,
\Delta
is live if and only if
\models\mathcal{F}
. As QBF is
PSPACE
-complete, we conclude that checking for liveness is
PSPACE
-hard.
∎
